To get to f6, the white pawn at f5 must move or be captured.
The white pawn can't move to f6. It could potentially move to g6? No, that's not possible.
Can it capture something on e6 or g6? No.

Wait, if the rook is at f4, it can't move to f6 because f5 is in the way.
The rook could move to f3, then f3-f6 is still blocked by f5.
So the rook must move out of the f-file to another file, then move to the 6th rank, then to f6.
Example: f4-d4-d6-f6.
But the black king is at d6. So f4-d4+ first, then once the king moves, the rook can potentially enter the 6th rank.
If f4d4+, and Black plays Ke6, the rook can't go to d6.
If f4d4+, and Black plays Kc6, the rook can go to d6?
Wait, if f4d4+ and Kc6, then Rd6+ is a check, and if Kd7, then Rf6 capturing the pawn.
So f4d4+ is a very strong move to start the process of infiltrating with the rook.

Let's double check:
1. f4d4+
If 1... Ke6:
The rook can't get to d6. Maybe Re4+? No, rook is at d4.
Maybe Rb4 to attack the bishop?
Or just wait.

2. Rd6+ (check)
If 2... Kc5, 3. Rd5+ (check) ...
If 2... Kb7, 3. Rf6 (takes pawn)

If 1... Ke7:
2. Rd6 (preparing Rf6)
If 2... Ke8, 3. Rf6.

If 1... Kc5:
2. Rd5+ (check)
If 2... Kb4, 3. Rd6 (preparing Rf6) or 3. Rf5? No.

Comparing f4d4+ and f4b4:
f4d4+ is a check, it forces the king. It's the first step to getting the rook to f6.
f4b4 attacks the bishop, but doesn't necessarily help in removing the f6 pawn.

Therefore, f4d4+ seems the most professional and forcing move.

- a4: occupied by Queen.
- a5: attacked (file a).
- a6: attacked (file a).
- b6: NOT attacked.
- c6: attacked (diag a4-e8).
- c5: NOT attacked.
- c4: attacked (rank 4).
- b4: attacked (rank 4).
So if 1. Qa4, the King (b5) can move to b6 or c5.
This shrinks the box to the b and c files and ranks 5 and 6.

If I move the Queen to d6:
Queen on d6 attacks:
- Rank 6: a6, b6, c6, e6, f6, g6, h6.
- File d: d1, d2, d3, d4, d5, d7, d8.
- Diagonals: c5, b4, a3; e5, f4, g3, h2; c7, b8; e7, f8.
Black King on b5:
- a6: attacked (rank 6).
- b6: attacked (rank 6).
- c6: attacked (rank 6).
- c5: attacked (diag d6-a3).
- b4: attacked (diag d6-a3).
- a4: NOT attacked.
- a5: NOT attacked.
- b5: not attacked (not on rank 6, file d, or diag).
Wait, the King is on b5.
If Queen is on d6, she does NOT check the King on b5.
The King's moves:
- a4: Legal.
- a5: Legal.
- a6: Attacked.
- b6: Attacked.
- c6: Attacked.
- c5: Attacked.
- c4: Legal (diagonal d6 to c5 is the only one).
Wait, diagonal d6: c5, b4, a3.
So c4 is legal.
So if 1. Qd6, King moves to a4, a5, or c4.

Let's try 1. Qd5+.
King's options: a4, a6, b4, b6.
If 1... Kb6, 2. Qd6+
- if 2... Kb7, 3. Qd7+ (Kb8, Kb6...)
- if 2... Ka5, 3. Qb5# (No, K takes Q).

Let's go back to the "shrink the box" method.
1. Qc4+
King moves to a5 or b6.
If 1... Ka5, 2. Qc5+
King moves to a4 or a6.
If 2... Ka4, 3. Qc3 (Box is a4-b5).
Now the king is very restricted.
Let's try 1. Qc4+.
